This state's existing rows on these laws stay; no clause text changed.

## IA topic gaps: end-of-run consistency pass (2026-11-13)

Pass run 2026-11-13 to 2026-11-14 in IA's chat, on the files staged 2026-11-13 from 1b69a10 (219 missing topics). This log is appended to `lease-clause-decision-log-IA.md` at sync. Pointers in rows read "gaps log §N" or "gaps log sN".

Pass date 2026-11-13. SOP v2.09. Corpus `corpus-IA-20261113`. Delta: `lease-clauses-IA-gaps-delta.csv`.

**Status:** topics covered 219 of 219 listed; 221 new rows, 17 tagged rows; 12 findings on existing rows; 0 for other states.

### §1 Sources, channel and currency

- **Channel.** Corpus `corpus-IA-20261113` (Claude Code, legis.iowa.gov, 2026-11-13: 2,303 requests, all HTTP 200), three parts, archive SHA-256 values matched `SHA256SUMS`; `verify_ia.py`: 423 files ok, 0 mismatches, 0 missing. The shell did not fetch from the official site; every statute in this pass was read from the corpus.
- **Step A.** `lease-clauses.csv` 67,456,983 bytes, SHA-256 `6b90f4fc…2773` (match); 17,484 rows, 17,372 active, 160 active IA rows (match); `missing-topics-IA.md` lists 219 topics (match). `/mnt/user-data/outputs/` held no earlier copies.
- **Texts held and how they were split.** Iowa Code 2026 official (47,239 sections, each with its history line); Iowa Code 2027 early release (27,098 headings; unofficial, includes the 2026 session); 2025 Iowa Acts (170 chapters) and 2026 Iowa Acts (201 chapters), split by act section (Section 1, Sec. 2, ...), with running page headers ("CH. 1002" lines) removed before searching; the Constitution, split by article and section (191 units); 11 Iowa Administrative Code chapters split by rule (169 rules; page running heads removed); Iowa Court Rules chapters 3, 16, 20 split by rule and form. The splitter was proved by count: 47,239 Code sections = the corpus index; every 2026 and 2025 Acts chapter present (201, 170); IAC rule headings matched only where a rule number is followed by its authority and a capitalised heading, which removed in-text cross-references that had split 481-280.14.
- **Currency.** Quoted wording comes from the 2027 early release where it differs from the 2026 official text; the 2026 text is the citation check. `sec.py` prints, for every section opened, both texts, whether they are identical (whitespace-collapsed), and every row of the 2025 and 2026 Code and Acts Sections Amended reports for that section; each row's notes record the result. Neither report lists any section of Iowa Code ch. 562A, ch. 562B, ch. 562 or ch. 648. No 2025 or 2026 extraordinary session.
- **Not in the corpus, not searched:** administrative rule chapters other than the 11 held; Iowa R. Civ. P. and court rule chapters other than 3, 16 and 20; case law; local ordinances (Des Moines, Iowa City, Cedar Rapids and Ames named where met, rule 3); federal text (cited only where an existing row already cites it or after a fetch).
- **The 2026-10-02 IA log was not among the attached files**, so its §18.2 statuses and §6.1 option list were not available as leads (Iowa item 2). Every topic was answered fresh from statute; the two declined options the kickoff names (`homestead-waiver`, `notice-to-quit-waiver`) were handled as item 2 asks.
- **Search engine.** `bat.py`: Python regular expressions, case-insensitive, `.` matching newlines, over whitespace-collapsed text with typographic quotes folded; proximity terms run in both orders within 1,200 characters and report a start-anchored section-wide AND in the same call; a heading-only screen, an inflection probe for every `\bword\b`, and the nonsense control `\bzqxv(?:ple|ort)\b` run in every call; every term needs a known positive (a read section or a synthetic sentence); the battery log records pattern, scope, unit count and a hash of the unit set. Citation lines in rows are generated by the tool, never typed.
- **Act-text flags (class decision 9).** Where a helper quoted a section the 2025 or 2026 session amended (Iowa Code § 216.8 by 2025 Iowa Acts ch. 1; Iowa Code § 714.16 and Iowa Code § 714H.3 by 2025 Iowa Acts ch. 25 and ch. 126 and 2026 Iowa Acts ch. 1078 and ch. 1188; Iowa Code § 10A.518 by 2026 Iowa Acts ch. 1115; Iowa Code § 631.6 by 2026 Iowa Acts ch. 1192), the quote checker confirmed each quoted passage in the 2027 early-release text as well as the 2026 text (it reports any quotation found only in the 2026 text; none was). No row describes what an amendment added or struck, so no act PDF had to be read for strike-through.
- **Browser channel.** The built-in browser reached uscode.house.gov (FEDERAL: 42 U.S.C. § 3603 read whole and saved; the `view.xhtml` links returned 404, the `/us/usc/t42/s3603/` path worked) and legis.iowa.gov. The Iowa Rules of Civil Procedure (Court Rules chapter 1, 10-30-2026) are served only as a PDF; the browser tools could not extract its text, and loading a PDF text extractor into the page was refused by the session's safety screen, so Iowa R. Civ. P. 1.241 was not read. The one row that touched it (`edu-rent-into-court-ia`) says the general court rules on counterclaims are not covered. The shell's own network could not reach legis.iowa.gov (proxy refusal), as in the 2026-10-02 pass.

### §4 Decisions and questions asked of Taylor

Questions asked of Taylor: none. Every judgment call was made under the kickoff's standing rules (Taylor's 2026-11-07 arbitration rule, the 2026-11-12 early-termination decision, SOP Part 5 for points only case law could settle); each is recorded below.

#### Iowa item confirmations
- **Item 8, shared rows.** `smoking-policy`: confirmed. `addendum-precedence`: confirmed. `keys`: confirmed. `hoa-compliance`: does not fit as written; override `hoa-compliance-ia` proposed in §6. `no-alterations`: confirmed against Iowa Code § 216.8A, and no Iowa telecom, solar or EV access statute conflicts (slice B11 decision below), with the caveat that its "unless Landlord requires its removal" sentence must not be used to require removal of an exterior disability modification (the restoration condition reaches only the interior: FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 100.203(a); Iowa Code § 216.8A). Each line is set out under "Decisions by topic".
- **Item 9(a).** `early-termination-ia` proposed in §6 (flat one-month fee, RECOMMENDED). **9(b).** `casualty-termination-ia` and `tenant-caused-damage-ia` name different persons; one wording for both proposed in §6. **9(c).** No IA eviction row contradicts `edu-cares-act-notice`; cross-references proposed in §6.
- **Item 10.** `arbitration-agreement` not tagged; `edu-no-lease-arbitration-ia` added (decision below).
- **Item 7 alarms.** Placement and equipment rules read whole (Iowa Admin. Code r. 481-280.15 to Iowa Admin. Code r. 481-280.17); additions for `edu-alarm-duties-ia` and `smoke-alarm-battery-ia` in §6.
- **`ev-charger-installation-hi` tag withdrawn.** Independent check round 1 found the B11 tag carried an Iowa doubt (shared-lot charging and Iowa Code § 452A.42(1)) only in its notes segment, which SOP rule 26 forbids; the Iowa variant `ev-charger-installation-ia` carries the cost cap and the shared-lot caution in its clause text (the round 1 erratum to that tag's segment is therefore moot).


#### Class decisions

Every quotation below was copied from the corpus and passes the quote checker. Open the section yourself (sec.py) before quoting any of it in a row.

1. **Waiver bar.** A rental agreement shall not provide that the tenant or landlord 'Agrees to waive or to forego rights or remedies under this chapter provided that this restriction shall not apply to rental agreements covering single family residences on land assessed as agricultural land and located in an unincorporated area' (Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a)). The other barred terms: 'Authorizes a person to confess judgment on a claim arising out of the rental agreement' (Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(b)); 'Agrees to pay the other party's attorney fees' (Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(c)); 'Agrees to the exculpation or limitation of any liability of the other party arising under law or to indemnify the other party for that liability or the associated costs' (Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(d)). Only (a) carries the farm-home exception.
2. **Knowing-use penalty.** 'A provision prohibited by this section included in a rental agreement is unenforceable' (Iowa Code § 562A.11(3)); for willful use of a lease 'containing provisions known by the landlord to be prohibited', the tenant 'may recover actual damages sustained by the tenant and not more than three months' (Iowa Code § 562A.11(3)) periodic rent and reasonable attorney fees. Quote around the possessive, never through it.
3. **Terms allowed.** 'The landlord and tenant may include in a rental agreement, terms and conditions not prohibited by this chapter or other rule of law including rent, term of the agreement, and other provisions governing the rights and obligations of the parties' (Iowa Code § 562A.9(1)).
4. **Common law supplements the act.** 'Unless displaced by the provisions of this chapter, the principles of law and equity in this state ... shall supplement its provisions' (Iowa Code § 562A.3). Penalty versus liquidated damages, unconscionability beyond Iowa Code § 562A.7, and most contract questions are case law. Case law is not in the corpus. Where a point turns only on case law, the row states the doubt and the cautious course and stays VERIFIED (SOP Part 5 NEEDS_REVIEW policy); its notes say "case law not searched for this row" (or name the case the 2026-10-02 pass read, only if an existing IA row already cites it).
5. **Mitigation and enforcement.** 'The aggrieved party has a duty to mitigate damages' (Iowa Code § 562A.4(1)); 'A right or obligation declared by this chapter is enforceable by action unless the provision declaring it specifies a different and limited effect' (Iowa Code § 562A.4(2)).
6. **Scope.** Iowa Code § 562A.5 lists arrangements the act does not govern (institutional residence, contract-of-sale occupancy, fraternal housing, 'Transient occupancy in a hotel, motel or other similar lodgings' (Iowa Code § 562A.5(4)), employee occupancy conditional on employment, condominium owners and cooperative proprietary lessees, agricultural premises, nonprofit transitional and homeless housing). Manufactured home communities and mobile home parks are Iowa Code ch. 562B: name that boundary in a row ("manufactured home community lots are governed by Iowa Code ch. 562B, not covered here") unless the topic turns on it, in which case read ch. 562B.
7. **Absence boundary sentence.** Every confirmed-absence row says what was searched: "Iowa's statutes (Iowa Code 2026, with the 2025 and 2026 Iowa Acts) and Constitution" plus, where run, the held administrative rule chapters or court rule chapters. Administrative rules outside the 11 held chapters, court rules outside chapters 3, 16 and 20, case law and local ordinances were not searched; say so in notes (and in the body where the body's claim would otherwise reach them).
8. **Local ordinances (rule 3).** Iowa cities run their own rental codes (Iowa Code § 364.17 requires larger cities to have housing codes and inspection programs; see `edu-rental-inspection-ia`). Name Des Moines, Iowa City, Cedar Rapids and Ames where a topic is local, but do not research or describe their ordinances. State preemption is real: Iowa Code § 364.3 limits what cities may do (read the subsection before relying), the rent-control ban (`edu-rent-control-ia`), and 2026 Iowa Acts ch. 1002 and 2026 Iowa Acts ch. 1200, § 40 (local civil rights ordinances, Iowa Code § 216.19).
9. **Currency.** No 2025 or 2026 act amends Iowa Code ch. 562A, ch. 562B, ch. 562 or ch. 648 (both Code and Acts Sections Amended reports). sec.py prints every amended-report row for a section: if it prints any, quote the 2027 early-release text, cite the act ("2026 Iowa Acts ch. 1115"), and don't quote changed words from the act's plain text (strike-through is lost); flag it in your topic object so the main pass reads the PDF.
10. **Federal law.** Cite as FEDERAL: 42 U.S.C. § 3604. Don't quote federal text and don't cite a federal section the library's existing rows don't already cite for this point, unless the main pass has fetched it: put a fetch request in the topic object instead ("fetch": ["FEDERAL: 42 U.S.C. § 3603(b)"]). A row can say "federal law may add duties in covered housing; not covered here" without a citation. Federal sections the main pass has fetched and saved are in `work/fed/` (file `42-USC-3603.txt` = FEDERAL: 42 U.S.C. § 3603); you may quote and cite those, and the quote checker reads them.
11. **Out-of-corpus primary sources** (other administrative rule chapters, Iowa R. Civ. P., other court rules): word the row so it is true whatever the source says, say in notes which source was not read, and list it in the topic object's "fetch" field. Don't put a "[[FETCH" marker in a row (the validator rejects it). The Iowa Rules of Civil Procedure (court rules chapter 1) could not be read in this pass (PDF only). The old Real Estate Commission rules (agency 193E) moved to agency 481: the corpus holds Iowa Admin. Code r. 481-2013 (trust accounts) and Iowa Admin. Code r. 481-2015 (property management) only.
12. **Existing IA rows stand.** Don't restate them as new rows; point to them by backticked id. A missing topic answered only inside another row still needs its own short row keyed to its own topic_key (rule 27). Defects in existing IA rows go in a finding_existing object, never in a new row.
13. **Declined and barred options (rule 54).** The 2026-10-02 log isn't available to this pass; answer from the statute. Known declined options: `homestead-waiver` and `notice-to-quit-waiver` (both not offered in 2026-10-02; each now gets an education row: lawful option not offered and why, landlord may add their own clause after advice; or the bar or the doubt quoted where it isn't lawful).


#### Decisions by topic

- `showing-notice-waiver`: Barred option (rule 54): a lease clause shortening the Iowa Code § 562A.19(3) 24-hour notice for showings would waive a right under Iowa Code ch. 562A, barred by Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a); the shared clause showing-notice-la is therefore not tagged for IA and no Iowa clause is offered; education row edu-showing-notice-waiver-ia records the bar.
- `notice-to-quit-waiver`: Rule 54: recorded as a barred option, not a lawful-but-declined one. The ch. 562A notices are rights under Iowa Code ch. 562A (Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a)); the Iowa Code § 648.3 notice to quit is served under Iowa Code § 562A.29A(1) and no statute lets a lease dispense with it, so the row treats a waiver as barred and states the residual doubt. No clause offered (consistent with the 2026-10-02 decision not to offer one).
- `homestead-waiver`: Rule 54: declined option, not barred. A homestead waiver is not a right under Iowa Code ch. 562A, so Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a) does not bar it, but a tenant has no homestead in the rented unit (Iowa Code § 561.1(1)), so the waiver usually reaches nothing; the library keeps the 2026-10-02 decision not to offer it and the education row tells the landlord to take advice and add a clause meeting Iowa Code § 561.21(2) (and Iowa Code § 561.22 for farm property) if wanted. No statute allows a lease waiver of the ch. 627 personal property exemptions; household goods are unreachable anyway (Iowa Code § 562A.31).
- `bed-bug-cooperation`: Tagged `pest-cooperation-pr` (optional clause, SERVES_LANDLORD) rather than writing an Iowa clause: it states Iowa law as written. Not tagged: `pest-cooperation-wy` (rests on a Wyoming statute in its last sentence) and the bed bug specific clauses of states with bed bug statutes. No separate education row: `edu-no-bed-bug-rule-ia` already states the absence of an Iowa bed bug statute.
- `pest-control-notice`: Education row only. No pesticide-notice clause offered: Iowa sets no landlord notice duty, and the 24-hour entry notice in `landlords-access-mi` already reaches indoor treatment; the treatment cooperation terms are in `pest-cooperation-pr`.
- `appliances-excluded`: Education row only; optional exclusion clause declined. `appliances-excluded-la` was not tagged: it rests on a Louisiana warranty-waiver article (its initials line), and its carve-outs imply that liability is limited outside them, which Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(d) bars. An Iowa clause disclaiming maintenance of an item left in the unit could be read as a waiver of the Iowa Code § 562A.15(1)(a)(4) duty, so the row recommends removing the item or listing it in `appliances-included` instead.
- `furnishings-included`: Tagged `furnishings-included-la` (optional, SERVES_LANDLORD); no separate education row, since Iowa has no furnished-rental rule and the tag segment states the absence with its battery. The clause's group (Security Deposit) is left as the shared row has it.
- `waterbed`: Tagged `waterbed-mn` (optional, SERVES_LANDLORD) instead of writing an Iowa clause or an absence row; the tag segment carries the battery. Its strict damage sentence is noted as a contractual extension beyond Iowa Code § 562A.17(6), lawful but subject to the general unconscionability check.
- `recycling-notice`: Education row only. No recycling clause offered: Iowa sets no recycling duty, recycling rules are local and vary by hauler, and the landlord can state sorting rules through `rules-ia` under Iowa Code § 562A.18.
- `smart-access`: Education row only. No smart-access clause offered: Iowa sets no rule, entry by code is already governed by `landlords-access-mi`, and key and code return is covered by `keys`; a clause promising a physical-key backup would add a landlord duty Iowa does not impose, so the body recommends it as practice.
- `designated-repairer`: Education row only; designated-repairer clause declined as barred in its usual form. The shared clauses (for example `designated-repairer-as`) forbid repair-and-deduct without landlord consent; in Iowa that would restrict Iowa Code § 562A.23(1)(a) and Iowa Code § 562A.27(4), a waiver Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a) bars, and a saving sentence does not cure a term whose core is barred. A plain repair-contact term adds nothing beyond `landlord-disclosure-ia`.
- `repair-notice`: Education row only. No separate repair-request clause: the IA-tagged `landlord-maintenance` already asks for prompt written notice, which Iowa allows as a tenant duty; a second clause would duplicate it. The row warns that a written-request term cannot defeat rights Iowa does not tie to a writing (Iowa Code § 562A.36(1)(b), Iowa Code § 562A.27(4)(b)).
- `periodic-services-entry`: Education row; `scheduled-services-entry-la` not tagged: it grants entry in addition to the entry section without the tenant's consent and lets the landlord fill in a notice period that may be under Iowa's 24 hours (Iowa Code § 562A.19(3)). An Iowa version keeping 24-hour per-visit notice was considered and declined as duplicating `landlords-access-mi`; the row says the option is lawful and that a landlord may add a schedule after advice (rule 54).
- `animal-care-access`: Tagged `animal-care-access-vi` (optional, SERVES_LANDLORD). Considered whether advance consent in the lease waives the 24-hour notice of Iowa Code § 562A.19(3); judged it is the tenant's own election for its own benefit, limited to an animal left without care (usually an emergency under Iowa Code § 562A.19(2)), not a waiver of the landlord-entry notice. The segment records that Iowa's animal-neglect and abandonment duties attach to custody, not to feeding for a set number of days.
- `home-business`: Education row; no separate home-business clause. Iowa lets the lease restrict business use (Iowa Code § 562A.20, Iowa Code § 414.33(2)(a)), and the restriction is already offered by IA-tagged `residential-use-only`; a permissive home-business clause would only remove the landlord's protection, so the row tells a landlord who wants to allow one to say so in writing with conditions.
- `farmland-hunting-rights`: Education row only. No hunting-reservation clause: the library's leases are residential, and farm leases are outside Iowa Code ch. 562A (Iowa Code § 562A.5(7)); the row tells an owner to reserve hunting rights expressly in the farm lease.
- `parking-rules-notice`: Education row only. No new parking clause: Iowa sets no parking-rules notice, and the IA library already offers `parking-ks-oh-ca`, `assigned-parking-space` and `parking-vehicle-rules-id`; the row points to them and to the Iowa Code § 562A.18 limits on rules adopted mid-lease.
- `children-occupancy`: Education row, not a clause: no Iowa statute requires lease text on children, and restating the familial-status ban is a landlord duty (education under rule 55). The existing `permitted-occupants` clause (tagged IA) already lists children as occupants, so no new clause is offered. A child joining mid-tenancy is treated as within Iowa Code § 216.8(1)(a)-(b) as the cautious course; case law not searched.
- `disability-accommodation`: Education row; no clause offered. Iowa lets a landlord condition an interior modification on the renter's agreement to restore 'where reasonable to do so' (Iowa Code § 216.8A(3)(c)(1)), a landlord right, but the reasonableness is judged per request, so a blanket lease clause pre-committing every tenant to restoration could misstate the test. The library already preserves lawful modifications in `no-alterations` (tagged IA) and the row tells landlords to write a per-request modification agreement. Rule 54: lawful option, not offered as a lease clause for that reason.
- `nonresident-owner-agent`: Education row; no clause. The owner-and-agent disclosure Iowa requires is already the `landlord-disclosure-ia` clause. The withholding question (Iowa Code § 422.16(2)(b)) is left as a pointer to tax advice because the Department of Revenue rules that apply it are not in the corpus. Kickoff item 12's four points are covered here, with pointers to `edu-entity-landlord-representation-ia` (B6) for entity representation.
- `law-enforcement-cooperation`: Education row; no clause. Kickoff item 12 point (4), reporting duties binding any person, is answered here by Iowa Code § 331.802(1) and Iowa Code § 523I.316(6); the landlord-specific duty found is court-ordered surveillance assistance (Iowa Code § 808B.5(5), Iowa Code § 808B.13). Whether a landlord's consent authorizes a police search is case law and stays a stated doubt with the cautious course.
- `telecom-access`: Tagged the shared `telecom-access-mn` clause (rule 26) rather than writing an Iowa row: Iowa has no telecom-access statute, so the contract-based clause states Iowa law as written. No separate education row, since the tag segment carries the confirmed absence and the battery; the federal antenna rule stays a fetch request.
- `portable-solar`: Education row for the tenant-right answer plus a tag of the generic optional `portable-solar-mo` clause (rule 26 and rule 54): Iowa has no solar statute reaching leases, and the clause's utility-approval and disconnection protection fits Iowa Code § 476.58(2)(c). The Iowa Utilities Commission interconnection rules are not held; listed as a fetch.
- `portable-cooling-device`: Education row plus an Iowa optional clause (rule 54): Iowa has no cooling-device statute, so window-unit consent and installation terms are lawful and useful (fall, egress and circuit risks). Neither existing clause was tagged: `portable-cooling-device-il` names Illinois law, and `portable-cooling-device-wy` could disclaim upkeep of supplied equipment, which Iowa Code § 562A.15(1)(a)(4) bars a lease from waiving.
- `ev-charging-shared-area`: Education row; the shared `ev-charging-shared-area-co` clause is not tagged because it presupposes a Colorado-style tenant entitlement and caps the landlord's response at a reasonable fee, which would understate an Iowa landlord's freedom to refuse or set terms. Landlords who allow shared-area chargers use `ev-charger-installation-hi` (tagged IA) with `assigned-parking-space`.
- `ev-charging-end-of-tenancy`: Education row; `ev-charging-end-of-tenancy-co` not tagged because it fixes ownership in the tenant by rule (Colorado's statute), which Iowa lacks, and it would conflict with the leave-in-place option in `ev-charger-installation-hi`, which Iowa now carries. The row tells landlords to allocate ownership in the lease because Iowa fixture law is case law.
- `no-alterations`: The shared `no-alterations` clause fits Iowa; no change and no finding. (1) Iowa Code § 216.8A: Iowa makes it discrimination to refuse 'at the expense of the person with a disability, reasonable modifications of existing premises' (Iowa Code § 216.8A(3)(c)(1)) and lets a landlord, 'where reasonable to do so', condition permission on the renter's agreement to restore 'the interior of the premises' (Iowa Code § 216.8A(3)(c)(1)). The clause's consent requirement is a rule the landlord must waive or bend for a qualifying request, and its last sentence ("does not limit any repair, installation, rekeying, or reasonable modification that applicable law entitles Tenant to perform") keeps that right; because the statute allows a restoration condition only for the interior, the clause's "unless Landlord requires its removal" must not be used to demand removal of an exterior disability modification, which the same last sentence also covers. `edu-disability-accommodation-ia` tells landlords this. Iowa has no lease-inclusion sentence for the restoration condition (IA-B11-005). (2) Telecom: no Iowa statute gives tenants an installation right (IA-B11-018); Iowa Code § 477.1 and Iowa Code § 477A.10 bind providers; the federal antenna rule is preserved by the last sentence. (3) Solar: no tenant right (IA-B11-019); Iowa Code § 564A.8 reaches only new-subdivision deeds by local option; the utility safety rules under Iowa Code § 476.58(2) support requiring consent. (4) EV: no tenant charger right (IA-B11-022, `edu-no-ev-charging-rule-ia`); the electric fuel tax and license (Iowa Code § 452A.41, Iowa Code § 452A.42) do not touch alteration consent. (5) Cooling: no tenant right (IA-B11-013, IA-B11-021).
- `political-access`: Education row; no clause. Iowa Code § 68A.406(1)(c) lets a lessee permit campaign signs on occupied leased residential space but does not address lease terms, so a lease ban is of uncertain effect; the row recommends neutral, content-blind limits rather than a ban, and the library offers no sign-ban clause for Iowa.
- `senior-housing-work-card`: Education row only: no Iowa duty exists, and staff screening is not a lease term, so no clause. Not tagged: other states' rows rest on their own statutes. The row carries the adjacent Iowa housing-for-older-persons exemption and the licensed-care boundary because a 55-plus landlord needs both. Iowa Code § 216.12 has 2025 and 2026 amended-report rows (paragraphs (1)(a) and (1)(e)); only paragraph (1)(d), unchanged, is quoted, so no act PDF is needed for this row.
- `student-housing`: Answered Present: Iowa's only student-housing rule is the scope exclusion for institutional residence incidental to an educational service and for fraternal housing (Iowa Code § 562A.5(1), Iowa Code § 562A.5(3)); like the California dormitory line, it is an exemption, not a duty, and reaches only housing incidental to education, so privately owned student buildings fall inside the Act. Education row only; no clause (a scope rule is not a lease term). Not tagged: other states' rows rest on their own statutes. Iowa Code § 262.9 has 2025 and 2026 amended-report rows; the quoted paragraph's words are unchanged and only its number moved (37 to 32), so the 2026 number is cited.
- `tenant-confidential-information`: Education row only. No consent-to-share clause offered: Iowa needs no tenant consent for ordinary business sharing, so the clause adds nothing for most landlords, and for the few landlords who are controllers under Iowa Code ch. 715D a blanket lease consent risks being void as a limit on Iowa Code § 715D.3 rights (Iowa Code § 715D.4(4)). The breach and data-security detail is placed in `edu-tenant-records-ia` (topic tenant-records) and pointed to here, one subject per row.
- `tenant-display-rights`: Education row only. A sign-and-flag lease clause is a lawful landlord option (Iowa Code § 562A.9(1), Iowa Code § 562A.18) but is declined as a new Iowa clause: the existing `rules-ia` clause already carries written property rules, and a blanket sign ban would collide with the lessee permission for campaign signs on occupied leased space in Iowa Code § 68A.406(1)(c), whose effect against a lease term is unsettled. The row tells the landlord to keep display rules content-neutral and to allow campaign signs in the tenant's occupied space. Not tagged: other states' flag rows rest on their own statutes.
- `tenant-employee-housing`: Education row only. An employee-housing clause (occupancy conditional on employment, vacate period on job end) is lawful but declined: an arrangement within Iowa Code § 562A.5(5) is an employment term outside the residential lease this library builds, and its enforceability after the job ends turns on case law; the row tells the landlord to use a separate signed employee housing agreement after advice. Not tagged: other states' rows rest on their own statutes (Delaware disclosure, Puerto Rico caretaker eviction, American Samoa guest workers have no Iowa counterpart).
- `tenant-records`: Education row only. No records-copy charge clause (as offered for Virginia): Iowa sets no tenant right to records, so a fee clause has nothing to price, and fee clauses invite the fee issues in `edu-fees-as-rent-ia`. Answered Present because Iowa's breach statute (Iowa Code § 715C.2) reaches a landlord holding applicant Social Security and bank data; the rent ledger part of the topic is confirmed absent with IA-B12-011.
- `tenant-right-to-organize`: Short education row keyed to the topic (rule 27), pointing to `edu-retaliation-ia`, which already states Iowa Code § 562A.36 in full; the new row adds only the organizing-specific points (no presumption for organizing, no affirmative meeting right outside Iowa Code ch. 562B, selective enforcement risk). No clause: the retaliation bar is a landlord duty implied by statute, so restating it would be education.
- `usufructuary-lessor`: Education row only. A life-tenant landlord disclosure clause (as Louisiana and Puerto Rico carry for usufructuaries) is declined: Iowa Code § 562.9 already fixes what happens to the tenancy, so the clause would restate a tenant protection (education, not a basis), and the one useful option, binding the successor, needs the remainder holder's own commitment under Iowa Code § 562.9(4), which the landlord cannot supply in the lease. The row tells the landlord to disclose the life estate in writing and seek that commitment. Not tagged: other states' rows rest on their own statutes.
- `veterans-incentive`: Education row only. A voluntary veteran rent concession is a lawful landlord option in Iowa (Iowa Code § 562A.9(1); veteran status is not a protected class under Iowa Code § 216.8), and the row offers it through the existing Iowa clause `rent-concession-ia` rather than a new clause. Not tagged: `veteran-servicemember-concession-ne` is titled for Nebraska and rests on Nebraska's 2025 fair housing safe harbour, which has no Iowa counterpart.
- `optional-lease-terms`: Rule 54: an option-to-purchase clause is lawful in Iowa but not offered; it brings sale law into the lease (seller disclosure under Iowa Code ch. 558A, with an unsettled trigger, and Iowa Code ch. 714F where the deal is a foreclosure lease-back). Education row `edu-lease-option-ia` says so and that the landlord may add their own clause after advice. The defaults inventory is education (rule 55): each default is already carried by an IA clause, so no new clause.
- `tenant-statutory-duties`: Bucket SERVES_LANDLORD (rule 55): the clause restates the Iowa Code § 562A.17 tenant duties as lease terms, which widens the Iowa Code § 562A.27(1) termination ground from noncompliance materially affecting health and safety to any material noncompliance with the rental agreement. RECOMMENDED, not REQUIRED: no statute requires the text. Not a tag: the WY, VA and HI duty clauses each differ from Iowa Code § 562A.17.
- `informal-dispute-resolution`: Rule 54: lawful option offered by tagging the shared optional clause `informal-dispute-resolution-or`. Its savings sentence preserves every statutory notice, deadline and remedy, so it is not a waiver barred by Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a); arbitration stays declined (`edu-no-lease-arbitration-ia`).
- `actual-notice-method`: Rule 54: a lease term adding text messages or similar methods for ordinary notices is lawful but declined as a separate clause. Iowa Code § 562A.8(1)(a)(6) and Iowa Code § 562A.8(1)(b)(6) already allow any method that results in actual receipt without agreement, so the clause would add only evidence value; `electronic-notice-ia` already carries the electronic-consent mechanics for e-mail. Education row `edu-actual-notice-method-ia` says the landlord may add their own term after advice, never for Iowa Code § 562A.29A or Iowa Code § 648.3 notices.
- `emergency-contact`: Rule 54: the optional tenant emergency-contact clause is lawful in Iowa and offered by tagging `emergency-contact-co`; no Iowa statute requires a landlord emergency number, so no clause on that side, only the education row `edu-no-emergency-contact-rule-ia`.
- `deposit-cost-schedule`: Education row, not a clause. Iowa Code § 562A.12(3)(a) limits withholding to amounts reasonably necessary for three reasons, with the burden on the landlord (Iowa Code § 562A.12(3)(b)); a schedule could only ever be an estimate, and a flat charge risks the waiver bar of Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a). Declined to offer a preset-charge clause; the deduction grounds already sit in `security-deposit-use-ia`. rule_type RECOMMENDED as an absence row.
- `deposit-installments`: Education row only. No Iowa statute creates or limits deposit installments; agreeing to them is the landlord's concession, not an option that serves the landlord, so no clause is offered. The row points out that `security-deposit-use-ia` requires the deposit before occupancy, so installments need an addendum, and that treating a missed installment as rent for the three-day notice is uncertain under Iowa Code § 562A.6(10).
- `deposit-last-month-rent`: Education row; no new clause. The landlord-serving term (tenant may not apply the deposit to rent without written consent) already sits in `security-deposit-use-ia`, and no Iowa statute gives the tenant that right, so the clause is lawful. The row warns that advance rent the lease lets the landlord use for damages is, on the plain words of Iowa Code § 562A.6(12), a deposit.
- `deposit-surrender-notice`: Education row; no new clause. The landlord-serving request for a written address already sits in `security-deposit-return-ia`. A clause making the refund depend on advance move-out notice or keys is declined because Iowa Code § 562A.12(3)(a) sets the only trigger and Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a) bars a waiver of the tenant's deposit rights.
- `expedited-deposit-disposition`: No optional clause. A Virginia-style expedited disposition (quicker refund for a waived itemization or a fee) would trade away the written statement Iowa Code § 562A.12(3)(a) requires and add a charge outside its grounds, so it risks the waiver bar of Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a); education row only.
- `fee-in-lieu-of-deposit`: Rule 54 declined option. No Iowa statute bars a deposit-alternative fee, so it is lawful as far as the corpus shows, but the library does not offer a clause: a landlord-held fee that secures performance may be a rental deposit under Iowa Code § 562A.6(12) (cap, holding and return rules) and a required charge is rent under Iowa Code § 562A.6(10); both points turn on the product and on case law. The row tells the landlord to take advice and add their own clause.
- `nonrefundable-deposit-notice`: Not tagged `nonrefundable-fees-wv` (rule 26): its trigger is an express written agreement that a fee is nonrefundable, which in West Virginia takes the fee out of the deposit definition; Iowa's definition (Iowa Code § 562A.6(12)) turns on purpose, not agreement, and the clause's pet-fee example could be a deposit in Iowa. Education row instead. Rule 54: a nonrefundable-fee clause for true fees is lawful (no Iowa bar) but not offered, because the line between a fee and a deposit turns on purpose and case law; the row tells the landlord to state each fee's amount and non-security purpose.
- `governmental-fines`: Not tagged `tenant-caused-fines-as` and no Iowa clause offered: a lease promise that the tenant will reimburse the landlord for a government fine imposed on the landlord reads as an agreement 'to indemnify the other party for that liability or the associated costs' (Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(d)), which an Iowa lease may not contain, with the knowing-use penalty in Iowa Code § 562A.11(3). The row instead points the landlord to damages for the tenant's own noncompliance (Iowa Code § 562A.17, Iowa Code § 562A.27(3)), stating the doubt. rule_type PROHIBITED because the clause is barred or doubtful.
- `government-fee-reimbursement`: No reimbursement clause offered (rule 54). A lease promise to repay a city inspection or enforcement fee charged to the owner may be an agreement to indemnify the landlord for its liability arising under law 'or the associated costs' (Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(d)); the plain words reach it and no case was read. The row recommends building the cost into rent or listing a fixed fee in `fees-and-charges-vi`, which is rent under Iowa Code § 562A.6(10) and subject to Iowa Code § 562A.13(5).
- `alarm-tampering-fee`: Not tagged `detector-care-la`. Its tamper ban and notice duty duplicate `smoke-alarm-battery-ia` (Iowa's alarm clause, keyed alarm-duties), with different notice wording; tagging would put two overlapping alarm clauses in the Iowa lease. Its cost-recovery sentence is lawful in Iowa (Iowa Code § 562A.17(6), Iowa Code § 562A.27(3)); the main pass may wish to add a similar sentence to `smoke-alarm-battery-ia` (no finding filed, as the existing row is not wrong). A fixed tampering fee is declined (penalty doctrine is case law, IA gaps log s4 class decision 4).
- `carpet-cleaning`: Not tagged `carpet-cleaning-ar` and no Iowa carpet clause offered. The clause deducts the cleaning cost as damages for lease noncompliance, but Iowa Code § 562A.12(3)(a) limits withholding to three grounds (rent and other funds due; restoration beyond ordinary wear and tear; bad-faith holdover possession costs), and a lease cannot enlarge them (Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a)). A version recast as restoration beyond ordinary wear and tear would add nothing to `security-deposit-use-ia`.
- `collection-fee`: Education row; collection-fee clause declined (rule 54). Iowa Code § 537.7103(5)(d) would let a fee the lease expressly authorizes be collected only if it is also 'legally chargeable', which turns on penalty doctrine (case law), and any part of the fee that is an attorney fee is barred by Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(c) with the knowing-use penalty of Iowa Code § 562A.11(3). The risk outweighs the benefit, so no clause is offered.
- `notice-service-fee`: No notice or eviction fee clause offered. No statute authorizes one; court costs follow Iowa Code § 625.1 and Iowa Code § 648.22; a fee covering legal work is barred by Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(c); a flat fee raises the penalty question (case law) and Iowa Code § 537.7103(5)(d)'s legally-chargeable test. Education row only.
- `subsidy-late-fee`: Education row (confirmed absence), not a clause. The existing `late-fee-ia` clause already states the landlord's own figures within Iowa Code § 562A.9(4); a tenant-share limiter would be a landlord-imposed restriction with no Iowa basis, so no clause is offered and the body gives the cautious course instead. Not tagged: other states' rows describe their own caps or absences. rule_type RECOMMENDED as an absence row.
- `tax-escalation`: Education row, declined option. A tax pass-through is rent (Iowa Code § 562A.6(10)) and Iowa Code § 562A.13(5) bars a rent increase taking effect before the end of the original agreement or a renewal; whether a lease-scheduled pass-through counts is unsettled. The library cannot draft a mid-term pass-through that works under every reading, so it declines one and says the landlord may add their own after advice. NOT TAGGED `tax-insurance-escalation-la` (20 states): its mid-term tax and insurance increase would collide with Iowa Code § 562A.13(5) under the reading the row treats as the cautious one.
- `rent-tax`: Education row, Present. The hotel and motel tax (Iowa Code § 423A.3, Iowa Code § 423A.4) reaches lodging of 31 days or less and, for rooming-house rooms, the first 90 days (Iowa Code § 423A.5(1)(b)); ordinary leases of a month or more are exempt and ch. 423 does not tax residential rent. No tax clause is offered: the library lease is for tenancies the tax does not reach, and a rooming-house operator's tax collection is a tax-compliance matter, not a lease term. Not tagged: other states' rows rest on their own tax codes.
- `rent-receipt-anti-waiver`: Education row, Present (purpose clause Iowa Code § 562A.2(2)(c)); the uniform act's operative separation section exists only in Iowa Code § 562B.12. Restating a landlord duty is education, not a clause. Group Landlord Responsibilities. Not tagged: other states' rows quote their own operative sections.
- `rent-installments`: Tagged `rent-installments-or` (optional, SERVES_LANDLORD). Iowa Code § 562A.9(3) makes the monthly-at-the-start schedule a default the parties may change; no Iowa partial-payment statute exists to conflict with the clause's definition sentence. No new row.
- `rent-escalation`: Education row; declined option (rule 54). A mid-term step clause may be a 'rent increase' that Iowa Code § 562A.13(5) forbids before the term ends, so no clause works under every reading; a renewal-rent clause adds nothing the 30-day written notice does not already do. Not tagged `rent-increase-midterm-ky` or `rent-escalation-mp`: both contemplate increases during the term. rule_type CONSTRAINED (the row explains a statutory limit on the landlord's choice).
- `rent-concession`: Optional clause `rent-concession-ia` (CONDITIONAL, SERVES_LANDLORD): a concession works only if the lease states it, and the credit-against-full-rent structure is the reading that survives the Iowa Code § 562A.13(5) doubt. Added a referral exclusion sentence because Iowa Code § 714.16(2)(b) voids the buyer's obligations where a price or rebate depends on referrals. No clawback offered (penalty doctrine is case law). Not tagged `rent-concession-wi` (reasons in notes). Companion education row `edu-rent-concession-ia` gives the landlord the referral and advertising warnings the clause body cannot.
- `rent-reporting`: Education row (confirmed absence). A rent-reporting clause is lawful in Iowa but declined: accurate reporting needs no lease term, and a reporting program brings federal furnisher duties the library does not cover; the landlord may add their own after advice. The row adds the Iowa debt collection limits (Iowa Code § 537.7103(1)(c), (3)(a)) that `edu-collection-fee-ia` already found can reach rent.
- `lockout-for-rent-delinquency`: Education row quoting the bar (rule 54, barred option): Iowa Code § 562A.33 allows possession only by action, abandonment or surrender, and Iowa Code § 562A.26 gives the tenant possession, damages, punitive damages and fees. rule_type PROHIBITED. Points to `edu-self-help-eviction-ia` for the general self-help rule.
- `property-tax-rent-disclosure`: Education row (confirmed absence of a landlord statement duty), with the Iowa Code ch. 425 rent reimbursement rules that bind landlords (Iowa Code § 425.33, Iowa Code § 425.35, Iowa Code § 425.36). No clause: nothing to disclose by lease. Fetch request for the department's rules in case the claim form requires a landlord statement; the body is phrased to hold either way.
- `unbundled-parking`: Education row (confirmed absence). No clause: the existing tagged `assigned-parking-space` and `parking-ks-oh-ca` already let the landlord state the space and terms; the row adds the Iowa rent-increase and rule-change limits that apply to a parking charge. Not tagged: other states' rows describe their own codes.
- `renters-insurance-rules`: Education row (confirmed absence). The requirement itself is already offered for Iowa by the tagged `tenants-property-insurance-ks-oh-ca`. Declined a force-placed coverage clause: the charge would be rent subject to Iowa Code § 562A.13(5) and a breach remedy already exists. Carrier choice is advice, not law (Iowa Code § 507B.5 reaches credit, not leases). Group Tenant Responsibilities.
- `landlord-liability-insurance`: Education row (confirmed absence at state level), naming the city housing-code route under Iowa Code § 364.17 without researching ordinances (class decision 8). No clause: a landlord's own insurance is not a lease term. Group Landlord Responsibilities.
- `tenant-insurance-claims`: Education row (confirmed absence). Declined a subrogation waiver or preservation clause: the subrogation question is case law, a waiver by the tenant of claims could fall under Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a) and a tenant indemnity under Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(d); the existing `tenant-caused-damage-ia` clause already preserves the landlord's damages for negligent damage.
- `subsidy-habitability-proration`: Education row (confirmed absence). Iowa's reduction rules (Iowa Code § 562A.23, Iowa Code § 562A.25) speak of the tenant's liability only; the subsidy side is federal contract law the library does not cover. No clause: a lease term allocating reductions could conflict with the housing assistance payments contract and, against the tenant, with Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a).
- `shutdown-rent-protection`: Education row (confirmed absence). No clause: a landlord's voluntary payment plan is a side agreement; the row points to the waiver-by-acceptance row for the reservation of rights.
- `guarantor-renewal`: Tagged `guaranty-as` (optional, SERVES_LANDLORD) and added companion education row `edu-guarantor-renewal-ia` for Iowa's surety statute (Iowa Code § 540.1, Iowa Code § 540.2), which a clause cannot safely waive while its reach to lease guarantors is unsettled. Not a new clause: the existing clause's renewal election already lets the landlord choose coverage.
- `dv-confidentiality`: Education row, not a clause: the Iowa Code ch. 9E duties fall on the landlord (accept the designated address, don't disclose it after written notice, don't display the participant's name) and no statute requires them in the lease. rule_type REQUIRED because Iowa Code § 9E.5(1), Iowa Code § 9E.5(5) and Iowa Code § 9E.7(4) impose duties; the cautious course for other victim paperwork is stated as advice. No optional clause: nothing here a lease may add for the landlord's benefit.
- `dv-deposit-timing`: Confirmed-absence education row. No clause offered: Iowa has no victim termination right whose deposit timing a lease could set, and a lease term letting the landlord hold a departing co-tenant's share past the statutory 30 days risks the waiver bar (Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a)); the cautious course (a written release agreement with every tenant that says how the deposit is handled) is given as advice.
- `dv-lockchange`: Confirmed-absence education row, no clause. A lease clause promising lock changes for victims would serve the tenant, not the landlord, and a clause letting the landlord exclude a co-tenant on a protective order would rest on a point no Iowa statute settles (Iowa Code § 562A.33, Iowa Code § 562A.26); the cautious course is given as advice. Lock cost: no statute; the victim compensation program (Iowa Code § 915.86(14)) is named.
- `dv-qualifying-documents`: Education row, not a clause: Iowa Code § 562A.27A(3)(b) puts the proof duty on the tenant only as a condition of a statutory exemption; a lease term narrowing what proof counts would risk the waiver bar (Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a)), so no clause is offered. The answer is Present for the single proof rule; the absence of any list, form or age limit is stated in the row and backed by IA-B5-009.
- `statutory-early-termination`: Education row listing Iowa's statutory tenant exits; no clause. Each exit is a tenant right a lease can't waive (Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a)), so restating them is education, not a lease term (rule 55). The fee point for the library's early-termination clause is the main pass's item 9(a) (finding on `early-termination-ks` in M.jsonl); this row only says no fee may attach to a statutory exit. Points to `edu-forfeiture-redemption-ia`, written later in this slice.
- `infirmity-termination`: Confirmed-absence education row. Rule 54: an infirmity early-exit clause would serve the tenant and is not offered; a landlord may agree one case by case. No clause.
- `repair-cost-termination`: Confirmed-absence education row. Rule 54: a clause letting the landlord end a fixed-term lease because repairs are uneconomic is declined: it would let the landlord escape the non-waivable Iowa Code § 562A.15 duty (Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a)), and no statute authorizes it. The existing optional `casualty-termination-ia` covers the casualty case and is pointed to.
- `environmental-event-termination`: Declined option (rule 54): a landlord environmental-event termination clause like `environmental-event-termination-mo` is not offered for Iowa and IA is not tagged on it. Iowa Code § 562A.25 gives only the tenant a casualty right; the library already offers the narrowed optional `casualty-termination-ia`, whose notes record the residual Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a) risk; extending a landlord exit to non-casualty events (contamination, government orders) widens that risk with no statutory footing. The row tells the landlord the option is not offered, why, and that they may add their own clause after advice.
- `landlord-remedies-termination`: Education row, no clause. The landlord's remedies are statutory and already reflected in `default-by-tenant-ia`; an acceleration clause is not offered (no statute, mitigation duty in Iowa Code § 562A.29(3), penalty doctrine is case law) and the row says so and points to `edu-no-liquidated-damages-rule-ia` (B1).
- `eviction-service-party`: Education row; declined option (rule 54): a lease clause naming a person to accept eviction papers for the tenant is not offered. Iowa Code § 648.5(2) lists the service methods and already makes a signed acknowledgment by any adult resident notice to all tenants; whether Iowa R. Civ. P. 1.305 would honor a lease appointment was not read (fetch). The landlord's own agent for service is already in `landlord-disclosure-ia`.
- `cold-weather-vacate-notice`: Confirmed-absence education row. Rule 54: a cold-weather pre-vacating notice clause would be lawful (Iowa Code § 562A.9(1)) but is not offered separately, because the IA-tagged `extended-absence-notice-ks` already uses the statutory option (Iowa Code § 562A.20) and covers winter absences; `cold-weather-vacate-notice-mn` is not tagged for Iowa (it recites a Minnesota criminal penalty).
- `notice-to-vacate-additional-terms`: Education row only. Iowa has no rule on added terms in a notice to vacate and no ban on work while a notice runs; the row points to the continuing duties (Iowa Code § 562A.15, Iowa Code § 562A.26) and the retaliation exception for code-required work (Iowa Code § 562A.36(3)(c)). No clause: a lease term about the content of a future notice would add nothing the landlord needs, and a move-out form that adds terms risks the waiver bar of Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a).
- `owner-move-in-reservation`: Optional clause offered by tag (rule 26, rule 54): Iowa leaves an owner-occupancy early termination of a fixed term to contract (Iowa Code § 562A.9(1)); the shared clause states nothing Iowa contradicts. Education row added for the confirmed absence and the retaliation limit (Iowa Code § 562A.36). IA-B6-002 was superseded by IA-B6-003 (inflection rerun for occupy). Not written as an Iowa clause of its own: the shared text needs no Iowa change, unlike Missouri, whose general statute reaches mobile home lots; Iowa lots sit in Iowa Code ch. 562B, outside the library.
- `good-cause-notice`: Education row only, pointing to the existing `edu-for-cause-eviction-ia` for the substance (IA gaps log s4 class decision 12). No clause: there is no notice to give, and a lease promise to end the tenancy only for cause would hand the tenant a right Iowa does not give.
- `automatic-renewal`: Optional clause offered (rule 54) with an education row. Not tagged on `automatic-renewal-ks` because its single {{renewal_notice_days}} figure can fall below the thirty days of Iowa Code § 562A.34(3) and Iowa Code § 562A.13(5). The Iowa clause holds the tenant to exactly thirty days: whether a longer tenant notice is a barred waiver under Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a) turns on case law not searched, so the clause is drafted to work either way. rule_type CONDITIONAL, is_default blank, basis SERVES_LANDLORD. IA-B6-005 was superseded by IA-B6-006 (inflection reruns for evergreen, extension, reminder).
- `tenancy-at-will`: Education row only. Iowa Code § 562.4 is present; for dwellings under Iowa Code ch. 562A the month-to-month and week-to-week rules of Iowa Code § 562A.9(5) and Iowa Code § 562A.34 apply, and the row gives the notice that satisfies both statutes (IA log s10.8 lists related pairs). IA-B6-007 was superseded by IA-B6-008 (inflection rerun for possession). No clause: the library lease always fixes a term, and `periodic-tenancy-notice-ia` already carries the periodic notice.
- `seasonal-tenancy`: Education row only. No seasonal clause: the library lease already fixes start and end dates, and Iowa gives a seasonal lease no different treatment. Iowa Code § 331.301 (county short-term rental rule) is named in notes but not quoted or relied on because sec.py printed 2025 and 2026 amendment rows for other subsections of it.
- `term-change-notice`: Optional clause offered (rule 54) with an education row. The clause couples an Iowa Code § 562A.34(2) termination notice with an offer of new terms, so it works whether or not Iowa would let a bare notice change a periodic tenancy. Fee increases are treated as rent increases needing Iowa Code § 562A.13(5) notice (cautious reading of Iowa Code § 562A.6(10)). IA-B6-010 recorded as failed (synthetic positive) and superseded by IA-B6-011.
- `tenant-bankruptcy`: Education row only. Iowa adds nothing to federal bankruptcy law beyond Iowa Code § 562A.3. Federal sections are cited in outline, unquoted, as existing tenant-bankruptcy rows cite them (class decision 10), and listed as fetch requests so the main pass can confirm or replace the outline with a shared federal row (the topic question notes a shared federal row is a candidate). No clause: an ipso facto bankruptcy default term is unenforceable after filing under federal law.
- `double-letting`: Education row only. No double-letting rule exists; the delivery-of-possession duty (Iowa Code § 562A.14) and the tenant remedies (Iowa Code § 562A.22) cover the double-booked unit. No clause: restating a landlord duty and a tenant remedy is education (rule 55), and a lease term limiting the remedy would be barred by Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a).
- `minor-tenant-filing`: Education row only, VERIFIED for what the statutes prove; the court-procedure part (service on and defense of a minor defendant) is worded to be true either way and sent to the main pass as a fetch request, since Iowa R. Civ. P. chapter 1 could not be read. No clause: an age warranty or misrepresentation clause would not stop disaffirmance on its own (Iowa Code § 599.3 turns on the minor's actual misrepresentation), so the row advises verification and an adult co-signer instead.
- `entity-landlord-court-representation`: Education row only. Iowa answers the topic by statute in small claims (Iowa Code § 631.14), with a court rule (Iowa Ct. R. 16.201(13)) that defers to it; outside small claims the answer turns on court rules and case law not in the corpus, so the row tells the landlord to use a lawyer and the topic lists fetch requests. The four kickoff item 12 points (foreign-entity suit bars, trade names, service on a nonresident owner, reporting duties) are covered here; the nonresident-owner-agent topic is another slice.
- `trespass-notice`: Education row only. The trespass statute works without a lease term; guest limits already sit in `guest-policy-ia` and `rules-ia`. The row flags the unsettled reach of a landlord ban to a tenant-invited guest ("without justification", Iowa Code § 716.7(2)(a)(2)) and steers the landlord to conduct-based common-area bans and lease enforcement. IA-B6-016 was superseded by IA-B6-017 (inflection rerun for consent).
- `pet-eviction-fact-sheet`: Education row only. No fact sheet or notice duty exists. The row adds the cautious course for animals left behind, pointing to the owner-side abandonment crime (Iowa Code § 717B.8) rather than the personal-property clause. No clause offered: a lease term letting the landlord dispose of a left pet would risk the animal-abandonment and neglect statutes, which were not read in full for this row beyond Iowa Code § 717B.8.
- `foreclosure-disclosure`: Education row only, recommending voluntary written disclosure as the cautious course (no statute requires it; misrepresentation exposure is case law under Iowa Code § 562A.3). No clause: a lease disclosure of a pending foreclosure would be case-specific and is better given as a separate written notice.
- `eminent-domain`: Optional clause offered by tagging `eminent-domain-vi` (rule 26, rule 54), with an education row for the absence and the tenant procedural rights. Iowa Code § 6A.24 is paraphrased, not quoted: sec.py printed a 2025 amended-report row for a vetoed addition to subsection 4 (class decision 9 caution); the subsection relied on, subsection 1, is unaffected by the veto, but the main pass may want to confirm. IA-B6-020 was superseded by IA-B6-021 (inflection rerun for rent).
- `adverse-proceeding-notice`: Education row only. The tenant forwarding duty that serves the landlord is already in the Iowa lease through `tenant-forward-proceedings-ca` (keyed tenant-forward-proceedings), so no second clause or tag is added; the row points to it (rule 57). IA-B6-022 was superseded by IA-B6-023 (its attorn stem matched attorney).
- `limitation-of-actions`: Education row only. No clause altering limitation periods is offered: since 2026 Iowa Acts ch. 1150, § 1 the periods in Iowa Code § 614.1 may only be altered by an Act of the general assembly, and a term shortening the tenant's time to sue could be a barred waiver under Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a). Partial-payment revival is not in the statute (Iowa Code § 614.11 requires a signed writing); the row advises written payment-plan acknowledgments.
- `landlord-registration`: Education row only. Iowa has no state registry; the city certification programs Iowa Code § 364.17 requires are local and are not researched (IA gaps log s4 class decision 8). No clause: a lease term cannot register a landlord, and the owner and manager disclosure is already the clause `landlord-disclosure-ia`. The state limits on local programs (Iowa Code § 414.1(1)(d), Iowa Code § 364.3(3)(d), (5)) and the broker licence line (Iowa Code § 543B.7(1)) are stated so an out-of-state owner knows what applies.
- `lease-registration`: Education row only. No Iowa statute requires recording or registering a residential lease. A clause barring the tenant from recording is lawful (Iowa Code § 562A.9(1); Iowa Code § 558.41(3) voids such clauses only in residential real estate installment sales contracts) but is not offered: a residential lease is rarely recorded, cannot be lawfully recorded without acknowledgment (Iowa Code § 558.42), and the clause's effect against a recorded lease is a case-law question. The row says the landlord may add one after advice.
- `single-family-zone-lease-limit`: Education row only. Iowa has no state limit on renting in single-family zones and no lot-split program; the state preemption rules (Iowa Code § 414.1(1)(b), (1)(d); Iowa Code § 364.3(23)(b)(4)) are stated because they answer whether a city may impose such a limit. No clause: nothing here is a lease term.
- `accessory-dwelling-unit`: Education row only. The accessory dwelling unit rules bind cities and counties, not lease terms. No clause: a rented unit is an ordinary dwelling unit under Iowa Code ch. 562A, and its shared-utility disclosure is covered by `utility-charges-disclosure-ia`. Flagged for the main pass: Iowa Code § 364.3(23) and Iowa Code § 331.301(29) were amended by 2026 Iowa Acts ch. 1013 (effective July 1, 2026); the row quotes the 2027 early release for the changed words.
- `health-district-rental-rules`: Education row only. No state duty to hand out health rules or refer tenants; local board of health rules (Iowa Code § 137.104(1)(b)) bind landlords and are flagged, not researched (IA gaps log s4 class decision 8). No clause: a lease term promising to supply local rules would add a duty the law does not impose.
- `lead-safe-certification`: Education row only. Iowa has no unit lead-safe certificate; its certification rule (Iowa Code § 10A.902) binds a landlord who renovates occupied pre-1978 housing, which the row states. The administrative rules are not held, so the row states no rule detail and the topic object requests them (IA gaps log s4 class decision 11). The existing federal clause `lead-based-paint` already carries IA.
- `lead-state-notices`: Education row only. Iowa requires no lead notice with a lease; the federal disclosure clause `lead-based-paint` already carries IA. The renovation pamphlet (Iowa Code § 10A.903), the sale-only lead service line disclosure (Iowa Code § 558A.4) and the lead program rules (Iowa Code § 135.102) are stated so a landlord does not mistake them for lease duties.
- `substandard-property-receivership`: Education row only. Iowa's receivership chapter (Iowa Code ch. 657A) applies only where a city or county opts in by ordinance and targets abandoned and nuisance buildings; repair orders are local under Iowa Code § 364.17 and Iowa Code § 364.12. No clause: these are government enforcement powers, not lease terms.
- `inspection-condemnation-disclosure`: Education row only; Iowa requires no disclosure of open code or condemnation orders. A voluntary disclosure clause was considered and not written: it would serve the tenant, not the landlord, and the row gives the cautious practice instead.
- `inspection-notice-penalty`: Education row only, pointing to the existing rows `edu-no-move-in-inspection-rule-ia` and `edu-deposit-return-penalty-ia`. No clause: an inspection clause would restate a practice, and Iowa attaches no penalty to omitting an inspection notice.
- `condemned-premises-rent-bar`: Education row only. The rent bar exists only as a city option under Iowa Code § 364.17(3)(a)(8), so the row names it and sends the landlord to the local ordinance (IA gaps log s4 class decision 8). For the main pass: slice B5's `edu-rent-demand-bar-ia` says no Iowa statute forbids collecting rent for a cited, placarded or unfit unit; that is literally true but omits the city ordinance authority in Iowa Code § 364.17(3)(a)(8) ('no rent shall be recoverable by the owner or lessee of any dwelling which does not comply with the housing code adopted by the city' (Iowa Code § 364.17(3)(a)(8))); consider adding a pointer to `edu-condemned-premises-rent-bar-ia` there.
- `sanitary-code-variance`: Education row only. Iowa's only housing code variance power is the city power in Iowa Code § 364.17(4), with no tenant notice. No clause: a variance notice clause would serve no landlord purpose the law requires.
- `certificate-of-occupancy-disclosure`: Education row only. Certificates of occupancy and rental certificates are issued under local ordinance (Iowa Code § 103A.19(2)(d); Iowa Code § 364.17(3)(a)); Iowa law requires no lease disclosure. No clause.
- `tpa-notice`: Education row only. Iowa has no tenant purchase right or notice of sale; the sale duties already have rows (`edu-deposit-on-sale-ia`, `edu-sale-management-change-ia`). A contractual right of first refusal or option for the tenant would serve the tenant, and the option clause is already declined in `edu-lease-option-ia`.
- `tpa-exemption-notice`: Education row only, short, pointing to `edu-no-tpa-notice-ia` (rule 57). Written after the tpa-notice topic in the answers file, out of the slice order; content unaffected.
- `tpa-sunset`: Education row only. No dated repeal on Iowa's landlord-tenant chapters; the row states that and points to the 2026 changes that touch local rental regulation.
- `subsidized-inspection-refusal`: Education row only. Iowa has no inspection-refusal rule; voucher acceptance is voluntary under Iowa Code § 364.3(16), already stated in `edu-source-of-income-ia`; the federal program inspection is covered by `edu-voucher-tenancy-addendum`.
- `repair-escrow-exemption-notice`: Education row only. Iowa has no rent escrow exemption or notice; rent into court (Iowa Code § 562A.24) and the city escrow option (Iowa Code § 364.17(3)(a)(5)) are stated so the landlord knows where escrow can arise.
- `special-district-representation`: Education row only. Iowa requires no special-district statement to tenants; the assessment pass-through question belongs to `edu-tax-escalation-ia`.
- `communal-land-lease-approval`: Education row only. Iowa has no communal land approval rule; the Sac and Fox settlement sections (Iowa Code § 1.12 to Iowa Code § 1.14) are stated because they leave trust land leasing to federal law. No federal citation (IA gaps log s4 class decision 10); the main pass may fetch the federal trust land leasing statute if the library wants to name it.
- `electric-submetering-disclosure`: Education row, no new clause: Iowa requires no submetering disclosure text; the only lease-stage duty is the oral full explanation in Iowa Code § 562A.13(4), already served by `utility-charges-disclosure-ia` (its bracket asks for how each charge is calculated). The master-meter cap in Iowa Admin. Code r. 199-20.3(1)(c) and Iowa Admin. Code r. 199-19.3(1)(c) binds the landlord's total charge; stated as education (CONSTRAINED). Whether a landlord who resells energy is a 'public utility' (Iowa Code § 476.1(2)) is case law not searched; the row states the doubt and the cautious course.
- `utility-apportionment`: Education row, no new clause. An apportionment term is lawful in Iowa (no bar found), but the library already gives Iowa the vehicle: `utility-charges-disclosure-ia` records the pre-signing explanation the statute requires and asks, for each service, how the charge is calculated, which is where a sharing formula belongs. A separate allocation clause would duplicate it. The water submetering rule (Iowa Admin. Code r. 199-21.3(1)(b)) is stated with its doubt and a cautious course rather than drafted around.
- `utility-interruption-submeter`: Declined option (rule 54): no planned-interruption or submeter-disconnection clause for Iowa. Iowa Code § 562A.26 and Iowa Code § 562A.33 bar willful interruption by the landlord, and a lease term excusing outages risks being read as a waiver of Iowa Code § 562A.23 or Iowa Code § 562A.26 remedies or an exculpation (Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a), (d)). The education row says the option is not offered, why, and that a landlord may add their own clause after advice.
- `utility-allowance-cap`: Optional clause written (`utility-allowance-ia`, SERVES_LANDLORD) instead of tagging `utility-allowance-cap-co`: the CO clause does not confine itself to a meter serving the unit alone, so in a master-metered Iowa building a per-unit overage could collect more than the utility billed, contrary to Iowa Admin. Code r. 199-20.3(1)(c) and Iowa Admin. Code r. 199-19.3(1)(c); and its "will not be characterized as Rent" sentence sits against Iowa Code § 562A.6(10). The Iowa clause is limited to a single-unit meter (modelled on `utility-allowance-cap-il`), adds no landlord charge, bars late fees on the overage and records the Iowa Code § 562A.13(4) explanation. Reused variable utility_allowance_amount.
- `fire-code-standard`: Education row only. No Iowa statute requires fire-code text in a lease. A tenant fire-safety clause like `fire-code-compliance-ut` would be lawful (Iowa Code § 562A.9(1)) but adds little in Iowa: tenants already must comply with code duties imposed on tenants (Iowa Code § 562A.17(1)), `fire-safety-grilling` (IA) covers open flames and flammables, and `rules-ia` lets the landlord adopt building rules. Not drafted for this slice; the row points to those rows.
- `heating`: Education row plus an optional tenant clause (`heat-freeze-protection-ia`, SERVES_LANDLORD), drafted fresh rather than tagging `heat-freeze-protection-ut` or `heat-freeze-protection-id` because both carry state-specific statutory references and charge formulas. The Iowa clause drops the absence-notice sentence (covered by `extended-absence-notice-ks`, which applies to Iowa) and keeps heat supply and heating repair with the landlord (Iowa Code § 562A.15(1)(a)(4), (6)).
- `window-guards`: Education row only. No lease clause offered: Iowa sets no window-guard duty or notice, and a tenant-rule against blocking or barring escape windows is already reachable through the rules clause (`rules-ia`) and Iowa Code § 562A.17(1); a voluntary guards-on-request clause would add a landlord duty without a statutory basis, so the body tells the landlord they may agree in writing instead.
- `pool-safety`: Optional clause `pool-use-ia` offered (SERVES_LANDLORD): Iowa sets no lease pool notice, so a pool-rules clause is the landlord's own term under Iowa Code § 562A.9(1); built on the structure of `pool-and-spa-use-il` without its Illinois statute references; no waiver or liability limit because Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(d) bars them.
- `association-obligations-disclosure`: Optional clause `association-rules-receipt-ia` offered (SERVES_LANDLORD): Iowa requires no association disclosure, but proof that the tenant received the association rules makes `hoa-compliance` enforceable; its notice-before-binding sentence follows the spirit of Iowa Code § 562A.18 for rules adopted mid-term.
- `construction-liens`: Optional clause `no-liens-ia` offered (SERVES_LANDLORD) instead of tagging `no-liens-ar`: the AR row lets the landlord recover "reasonable costs" caused by a lien, which could be read to include attorney fees that Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(c) bars; the Iowa clause limits recovery to actual damages not including attorney fees and uses Iowa's word "posted" for liens (Iowa Code § 572.8 posting to the registry).
- `nuisance`: No new nuisance clause: `residential-use-only` (no illegal purpose), `no-disturbance` and `criminal-activity-ia` already give the landlord the lease breach needed to act on Iowa Code § 99.1A, Iowa Code § 657.2(6) and Iowa Code § 725.4 conduct; a separate clause would duplicate them.
- `drug-free-housing-addendum`: No separate addendum clause: `criminal-activity-ia` already serves as Iowa's drug and crime lease term and tracks Iowa Code § 562A.27A; a second addendum would duplicate it and risk wording that waives Iowa Code § 562A.27A(3) exemptions or limits Iowa Code § 562A.27B rights.
- `association-obligations-disclosure`: After reading the main pass's finding on `hoa-compliance` (proposed override `hoa-compliance-ia`), errata align `association-rules-receipt-ia` with Iowa Code § 562A.18(2) (reasonable notice and no substantial modification for later rules). If `hoa-compliance-ia` is adopted, the two clauses work together: the override states the compliance duty and fine pass-through, `association-rules-receipt-ia` lists the documents handed over. If the main pass prefers one clause, `association-rules-receipt-ia` can be dropped without loss of any Iowa requirement.
- `binding-arbitration`: Arbitration (Iowa item 10): `arbitration-agreement` is not tagged IA. Iowa Code § 679A.1(2)(a) excludes 'A contract of adhesion' (Iowa Code § 679A.1(2)(a)) and no Iowa statute defines one, so whether a form lease is one turns only on case law; and a pre-dispute arbitration term may be an Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a) waiver of remedies carrying the Iowa Code § 562A.11(3) penalty. Either ground alone takes the clause out under Taylor's 2026-11-07 rule. The clause's carve-outs (possession actions, small claims) would fit Iowa Code ch. 648 and Iowa Code ch. 631; that is moot. New row `edu-no-lease-arbitration-ia` records the decline and the post-dispute route (Iowa Code § 679A.1(1)).
- `smoking-policy`: Shared row `smoking-policy` (Iowa item 8): confirmed. The tenant pays only remediation cost "caused by smoking by Tenant, an occupant, or a guest or invitee of Tenant in violation of this Section"; that is damages for breach of a lease term (Iowa Code § 562A.9(1), Iowa Code § 562A.27(3)), not an indemnity or exculpation term (Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(d)). It reaches a guest's smoking without the tenant's knowledge, beyond the statutory duty not to 'knowingly permit a person to do so' (Iowa Code § 562A.17(6)), but as a contract term the act allows. It touches neither tenant-fault exception (Iowa Code § 562A.21(1)(c), Iowa Code § 562A.23(3)), which limit the tenant's own remedies, not the tenant's liability.
- `addendum-precedence`: Shared row `addendum-precedence` (Iowa item 8): confirmed. Its exception ("any disclosure, notice, or addendum that applicable law requires ... will control") keeps `landlord-disclosure-ia` (Iowa Code § 562A.13(1)), `superfund-disclosure-ia` (Iowa Code § 562A.13(6)), `utility-charges-disclosure-ia` (Iowa Code § 562A.13(4)) and the federal lead disclosure in control. The Iowa Code § 562A.27A(1) notice is a termination-time notice (a 'three days' written notice of termination and notice to quit' setting forth 'the language of subsection 3' (Iowa Code § 562A.27A(1))), not a lease addendum, so the clause cannot conflict with it.
- `keys`: Shared row `keys` (Iowa item 8): confirmed. "Landlord may re-key the applicable locks and charge the cost to Tenant" applies only when Tenant fails to return keys or needs a replacement: actual cost of the tenant's own failure, recoverable as damages (Iowa Code § 562A.27(3)); it is not an agreement to pay attorney fees (Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(c)), and it is a lease term, not a later rule, so Iowa Code § 562A.18(2) does not reach it.
- `hoa-compliance`: Shared row `hoa-compliance` (Iowa item 8): does not fit as written. It binds the tenant to association rules adopted after signing with neither the notice nor the no-substantial-modification condition of Iowa Code § 562A.18(2), and to existing rules without the notice-at-signing condition of Iowa Code § 562A.18(1)(f). Override `hoa-compliance-ia` proposed under s6 findings (Claude Code applies at sync).
- `optional-lease-terms`: Rule 57 screen: `optional-lease-terms` carries two Iowa education rows, `edu-optional-lease-terms-ia` (the general list of lawful optional terms) and `edu-lease-option-ia` (leases with an option to buy, not offered). Kept as two rows: they are different subjects, and the library files other states' option-to-buy rows under the same key (three rows titled with an option to buy are keyed `optional-lease-terms`), so no separate key exists to move it to.
- `ev-charging-requirements`: Independent check round 1 found that the B11 tag of `ev-charger-installation-hi` carried an Iowa doubt (Iowa Code § 452A.42(1), shared-lot charging) only in its notes segment. SOP rule 26 bars carrying a condition only in notes, so the tag is withdrawn and the Iowa variant `ev-charger-installation-ia` puts the cost cap and the shared-lot caution in the clause text.

### §5 Independent check

Rule 80, scaled as the kickoff asks: at most two rounds, one checking agent at a time, the second round re-checking only rows edited after the first. The checkers had not written the rows; each read the statutes itself (`sec.py`), could run its own batteries, and returned findings as exact old-to-new errata, which the main pass applied with a once-only assertion after reading each one (errata files are kept with the pass and applied in the order written).

- **Round 1, part A** (main pass, B1, B2, B3: 58 rows, 4 tags): 0 ERROR, 25 FIX. Chiefly the willful-use penalty stated as if damages were capped at three months' rent (13 rows); two rows contradicting each other on whether that penalty reaches an over-cap late fee (both now say it is unsettled, cautious course: assume it can); dropped statutory conditions (Iowa Code § 562A.27(4)(d), "deliberately or negligently" in Iowa Code § 562A.23(1), "other funds due" in Iowa Code § 562A.12(3)(a)(1)); three absence claims narrowed to the chapters searched; one rule_type REQUIRED changed to PROHIBITED. Checker battery IA-CHK1A-001 confirmed the no-habitability-presumption claim. Main-pass sweep afterwards: no other row states the penalty as a cap.
- **Round 1, part B** (B4-B7: 74 rows, 4 tags): 1 ERROR, 14 FIX. ERROR: `edu-guarantor-renewal-ia` omitted Iowa Code § 540.2's condition that the creditor give the surety a true copy of the contract and the use of the original. FIXes included the scope of Iowa Code § 562A.21(1), the servicemember exit's source (Iowa Code § 29A.101A, outside the ch. 562A waiver bar), the 31-day hotel tax exemption, Iowa Code § 654.14(2), the real-property carve-out of Iowa Code § 9E.5(6), and the farm-home exception to Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a). Main-pass sweep S1 then added that exception to 11 more rows stating the bar without it.
- **Round 1, part C** (B8-B12: 88 rows, 10 tags): 1 ERROR, 27 FIX. ERROR: `edu-energy-data-release-ia` read the energy-benchmarking bar as reaching reporting ordinances; the statutory definition (Iowa Code § 414.1(1)(g)) reaches only reduction mandates. FIXes included Iowa Code § 99.27 and § 99.31 (mulct tax), Iowa Code ch. 657A's statewide reach, Iowa Code § 206.19(3), Iowa Code § 103A.10, Iowa Code § 562A.27B(2), Iowa Code § 562A.28, and "doing business" in Iowa Code ch. 489 and ch. 490. The checker also found that the B11 tag of `ev-charger-installation-hi` carried an Iowa condition only in its notes; the tag was withdrawn and `ev-charger-installation-ia` written (SOP rule 26). Main-pass sweep S2: farm-home exception in 2 more rows; repair-and-deduct and essential-services conditions in 2 rows.
- **Round 2** (rows edited after round 1, plus the new `ev-charger-installation-ia`: 70 rows, 1 tag): 0 ERROR, 14 FIX, all applied. It caught an additional-insured requirement in the new EV clause that could read as indemnity (Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(d); reworded to the tenant's own liability coverage), stale pointers to the withdrawn tag in three EV rows, an overbroad round-1 rewording of `edu-no-tpa-notice-ia` (checker battery IA-CHK2X-001 found Iowa Code § 6B.56), a round-1 edit to `edu-no-repair-cost-termination-ia` that contradicted the other habitability rows, and three rows where the sweep's farm-home sentence suggested a term another rule still bars.
