- **Edited after the last round** (round 2's own fixes, not re-checked by a further round; Claude Code reads them against the statute at sync): `edu-actual-notice-method-ia`, `edu-appliances-excluded-ia`, `edu-ev-charging-requirements-ia`, `edu-late-fee-limit-ia`, `edu-limitation-of-actions-ia`, `edu-no-ev-charging-end-of-tenancy-ia`, `edu-no-ev-charging-shared-area-ia`, `edu-no-repair-cost-termination-ia`, `edu-no-tpa-notice-ia`, `edu-nonresident-owner-agent-ia`, `edu-utility-interruption-submeter-ia`, `ev-charger-installation-ia`.


### §6 Findings on existing rows

- `edu-towing-ia`: Noticed while answering parking-rules-notice (not searched for). The row's notes cite the 2026 towing amendments as "2026 Iowa Acts ch. 1182, §§ 1-6 (HF 2617, effective July 1, 2026; read as enrolled)", which uses a bill number and a bare section range (cite as 2026 Iowa Acts ch. 1182, § 1 through 2026 Iowa Acts ch. 1182, § 6). The notes also quote, in single quotes, words read from the act's plain text with strike-through lost (the notice period quoted with both "twenty" and "ten", and the reclaiming period quoted with both "ten-day" and "twenty-day"), which IA gaps log s4 class decision 9 says not to do: quote the 2027 early-release text of Iowa Code § 321.89 instead, after opening it section-open. Not checked here: whether the body's 10-day and 20-day figures match the 2027 early release.
- `edu-no-dv-termination-ia`: The body says "Iowa protects victims in two ways" (the clear-and-present-danger exemption and the emergency-assistance right), but the address confidentiality program also binds landlords: 'If a program participant has notified the program participant's landlord in writing that the individual is a program participant pursuant to this chapter, a local ordinance or the landlord shall not allow the display of the program participant's name at an address otherwise protected under this chapter' (Iowa Code § 9E.7(4)), and a person notified on the Secretary of State's form 'shall not knowingly disclose the program participant's address' (Iowa Code § 9E.5(5)). Proposed: replace "Iowa protects victims in two ways:" with "Iowa protects victims in three ways:" and append "; and a tenant enrolled in the Secretary of State's address confidentiality program can require you to accept a substitute address and not to disclose or display their address and name (`edu-dv-confidentiality-ia`)." to the sentence that lists the protections, adjusting its punctuation; also add pointers to `edu-no-dv-lockchange-ia`, `edu-no-dv-deposit-timing-ia` and `edu-dv-qualifying-documents-ia`.
- `edu-dv-eviction-protection-ia`: Its notes list the order chapters as "chapters 235F, 236, 598, 664A, 915 orders", matching Iowa Code § 562A.27A(3)(a)(1), but sexual abuse protective orders under Iowa Code ch. 236A (enacted 2017) are not named there and qualify only through 'or any other applicable provision' (Iowa Code § 562A.27A(3)(a)(1)). Proposed notes addition: "A sexual abuse protective order under Iowa Code ch. 236A is covered by the catch-all words (`edu-dv-protection-order-citations-ia`)." Body unchanged (it already says "a protective or no-contact order").
- `periodic-tenancy-notice-ia`: The clause lets the landlord set one notice period for both parties: "either Landlord or Tenant may end it by written notice given to the other at least [state the notice period: at least 30] days before the periodic rental date specified in the notice". Iowa Code § 562A.34(2) says 'The landlord or the tenant may terminate a month-to-month tenancy by a written notice given to the other at least thirty days prior to the periodic rental date specified in the notice' (Iowa Code § 562A.34(2)), and a rental agreement shall not provide that the tenant 'Agrees to waive or to forego rights or remedies under this chapter' (Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a)). If a landlord fills in more than 30, the clause makes the tenant give more notice than the statute requires, which a court could read as a waiver of the tenant's statutory right to leave on thirty days; no Iowa authority read in this pass decides it (case law not searched). Proposed wording, which works under every reading: "If this Lease continues as a month-to-month tenancy, Landlord may end it by written notice given to Tenant at least [state the notice period: at least 30] days before the periodic rental date specified in the notice, and Tenant may end it by written notice given to Landlord at least 30 days before the periodic rental date specified in the notice." (and the same split for the week-to-week sentence, with Tenant's period fixed at 10 days under Iowa Code § 562A.34(1)). The new `automatic-renewal-ia` (B6) is drafted this way, so the two rows would then match.
- `utility-charges-disclosure-ia`: The notes say "No Iowa statute regulates submetering or ratio billing for residential rentals outside mobile home parks". True of statutes, but the Utilities Commission rules held in the corpus limit allocation of master-metered gas and electricity: 'if some other method of allocating the cost of the electric service is used, the total charge for electric service shall not exceed the total electric bill charged by the utility for the same period' (Iowa Admin. Code r. 199-20.3(1)(c)); the gas rule is the same (Iowa Admin. Code r. 199-19.3(1)(c), version effective December 16, 2026). The clause's bracket invites "any administrative fee" for each charge. Proposed: add to the bracket "[for gas or electricity from a master meter serving more than one unit, the total charged to all tenants may not exceed the utility's bill for the same period]" and add to the notes: "Master-metered gas and electricity: Iowa Admin. Code r. 199-20.3(1)(c) and Iowa Admin. Code r. 199-19.3(1)(c) cap the total charge at the utility's bill (`edu-electric-submetering-ia`)."
- `early-termination-ks`: Iowa item 9(a): IA is tagged on `early-termination-ks`, whose fee is "one month's Rent or 30% of the remaining Rent, whichever is greater". Late in a long term the greater-of formula can exceed any reasonable forecast of the landlord's loss, and Iowa imposes a mitigation duty when a tenant leaves: 'If the tenant abandons the dwelling unit, the landlord shall make reasonable efforts to rent it at a fair rental' (Iowa Code § 562A.29(3)). Proposed: add `early-termination-ia` (below; flat one-month fee, RECOMMENDED per Taylor 2026-11-12) and remove IA from `early-termination-ks` at sync.

If Tenant gives that notice, pays the fee and delivers possession of the property by the termination date, Tenant owes no Rent for any period after the termination date. If Tenant leaves before the end of the Term without using this option, Tenant's liability is governed by Iowa law, including Landlord's duty to make reasonable efforts to rent the property to someone else at a fair rental. This early-termination option and fee apply only if this Lease has a fixed Term; a month-to-month or week-to-week tenancy ends on the notice that Iowa law and this Lease provide, without an early termination fee. Landlord may terminate this Lease early only as this Lease's Tenant Default and notice provisions and Iowa law allow, and will regain possession only through a court proceeding. Nothing in this Section limits any right either party has under applicable law, and no early termination fee is owed when Tenant ends this Lease under such a right, including Tenant's right to terminate because of military service under the Servicemembers Civil Relief Act or Iowa law, Tenant's right to terminate after a fire or casualty, and Tenant's right to terminate because Landlord has not complied with this Lease or Iowa law.; rule_type: RECOMMENDED; content_type: LEASE_CLAUSE; verification_status: VERIFIED; notes: IA: Overrides `early-termination-ks` for Iowa (remove IA from `early-termination-ks` when this row is added). Flat one-month fee, as `early-termination-mo`, `early-termination-id` and `early-termination-in`, instead of the greater of one month or 30% of remaining Rent: no Iowa statute governs a lease early-termination fee (IA gaps log s4), and the landlord's mitigation duty applies when a tenant simply leaves: 'If the tenant abandons the dwelling unit, the landlord shall make reasonable efforts to rent it at a fair rental' (Iowa Code § 562A.29(3)); 'The aggrieved party has a duty to mitigate damages' (Iowa Code § 562A.4(1)). Penalty versus liquidated damages is Iowa case law, not searched; a flat one-month fee for an option the tenant chooses is the cautious figure (IA gaps log s4 class decision 4). Iowa has no domestic-violence termination right (`edu-no-dv-termination-ia`), so the Missouri model's domestic-violence fee sentence is dropped. - `casualty-termination-ia`: Iowa item 9(b): the two clauses drafted together name different persons whose fault counts. `casualty-termination-ia` preserves liability for damage caused by "the deliberate or negligent act or omission of Tenant or anyone on the property with Tenant's consent"; `tenant-caused-damage-ia` covers "the deliberate or negligent act or omission of Tenant, an Occupant, or a guest or invitee of Tenant". Iowa's own fault list is 'the deliberate or negligent act or omission of the tenant, a member of the tenant's family, or other person on the premises with the tenant's consent' (Iowa Code § 562A.21(1)(c)), repeated in Iowa Code § 562A.23(3) ('the tenant, a member of the tenant's family, or other person on the premises with the consent of the tenant' (Iowa Code § 562A.23(3))); Iowa Code § 562A.36(3)(a) uses 'the tenant or other person in the tenant's household or upon the premises with the tenant's consent' (Iowa Code § 562A.36(3)(a)). Iowa Code § 562A.25 itself has no fault exception, so the persons matter only to liability, but SOP rule 54 (UT circle-back 2) wants identical persons covering the statute's list. Proposed errata, applied to both rows: in `casualty-termination-ia` replace "of Tenant or anyone on the property with Tenant's consent" with "of Tenant, a member of Tenant's family or household, an Occupant, or a guest, invitee or other person on the property with Tenant's consent"; in `tenant-caused-damage-ia` replace "of Tenant, an Occupant, or a guest or invitee of Tenant" with the same words. Liability for a person who is on the property without Tenant's consent stays outside both clauses, which keeps them within Iowa Code § 562A.17(6) ('knowingly permit a person to do so' (Iowa Code § 562A.17(6))) and the statute's consent test.
- `edu-nonpayment-notice-ia`: Iowa item 9(c): no IA eviction row contradicts `edu-cares-act-notice`. Of the eight rows checked (`edu-nonpayment-notice-ia`, `edu-noncompliance-cure-ia`, `edu-clear-present-danger-ia`, `edu-eviction-process-ia`, `edu-for-cause-eviction-ia`, `edu-end-of-term-ia`, `default-by-tenant-ia`, `criminal-activity-ia`) only `edu-nonpayment-notice-ia` mentions the CARES Act, saying "The Iowa Supreme Court has held that the federal CARES Act's 30-day notice applies only to rent defaults that arose during the 2020 moratorium" (MIMG CLXXII Retreat on 6th, LLC v Miller, 16 N.W.3d 489 (Iowa 2025), as that row's notes record). The shared row says "Courts disagree about whether it still applies" and gives the safe course of 30 days' notice for a covered property; the two are consistent, but neither points to the other, and the shared row carries no IA segment. Proposed (Claude Code at sync): add an IA segment to `edu-cares-act-notice`: "IA: Iowa's state courts follow MIMG CLXXII Retreat on 6th, LLC v Miller, 16 N.W.3d 489 (Iowa 2025): the FEDERAL: 15 U.S.C. § 9058(c)(1) notice reaches only rent defaults that arose during the moratorium (`edu-nonpayment-notice-ia`). Federal courts are not bound by that reading."; and append to `edu-nonpayment-notice-ia`'s body: "Courts elsewhere read the federal notice more broadly; see `edu-cares-act-notice`."
- `hoa-compliance`: Iowa item 8 (`hoa-compliance` check): the shared clause "Tenant will comply with the association's rules and regulations applicable to the property" binds the tenant to every association rule, including rules adopted after signing, but Iowa enforces a rule against a tenant only if 'it is written' (Iowa Code § 562A.18(1)) and 'The tenant has notice of it at the time the tenant enters into the rental agreement' (Iowa Code § 562A.18(1)(f)), and a later rule only 'if reasonable notice of its adoption is given to the tenant and it does not work a substantial modification of the rental agreement' (Iowa Code § 562A.18(2)). Proposed override `hoa-compliance-ia` (below); remove IA from `hoa-compliance` at sync.
  - Proposed row `hoa-compliance-ia`: group: Disclosures; title: Homeowner / Condominium Association Compliance; states: IA; is_active: TRUE; supersedes: hoa-compliance; bodyText: If the property is in a homeowners or condominium association, Tenant will comply with the association's declaration, bylaws, rules and regulations that apply to the property and are in writing, a copy of whose rules and regulations Landlord has given Tenant before Tenant signs this Lease. A rule the association adopts or changes after Tenant signs this Lease binds Tenant only after Landlord gives Tenant reasonable notice of it, and only if it does not work a substantial modification of this Lease. A violation of a rule that binds Tenant under this Section by Tenant, an Occupant, or a guest or invitee of Tenant is a breach of this Lease. If the association notifies Landlord of a violation, Landlord will promptly give Tenant a copy of the notice. If the association lawfully imposes a fine on Landlord because of the violation, Tenant will pay Landlord, as Landlord's damages for the breach, the amount of the fine, with no markup or added fee, and Landlord will give Tenant a copy of the association's notice of the fine when Landlord charges Tenant. This Section does not make Tenant pay any association dues or assessment.; rule_type: RECOMMENDED; content_type: LEASE_CLAUSE; verification_status: VERIFIED; notes: IA: Overrides `hoa-compliance` for Iowa (remove IA from `hoa-compliance` when this row is added). Iowa's rules statute reaches rules 'however described, concerning the tenant's use and occupancy of the premises' (Iowa Code § 562A.18(1)); a rule binds only if 'it is written' (Iowa Code § 562A.18(1)) and 'The tenant has notice of it at the time the tenant enters into the rental agreement' (Iowa Code § 562A.18(1)(f)), and 'A rule adopted after the tenant enters into the rental agreement is enforceable against the tenant if reasonable notice of its adoption is given to the tenant and it does not work a substantial modification of the rental agreement' (Iowa Code § 562A.18(2)). The shared clause binds the tenant to all association rules, including later ones, with neither condition. Whether an association's rules passed through by the lease are the landlord's 'rules' under Iowa Code § 562A.18 is not settled (case law not searched); the clause is drafted to work either way (IA gaps log s4 class decision 4). Fines: framed as damages for the tenant's breach (Iowa Code § 562A.27(3)), not an agreement 'to indemnify the other party for that liability or the associated costs' (Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(d)); the residual risk the existing IA segment records (fines are levied on the owner under the declaration) remains, and the no-markup, copy-of-notice wording narrows it. - `edu-alarm-duties-ia`: Iowa item 7 (alarm placement rules, read whole: Iowa Admin. Code r. 481-280.15, Iowa Admin. Code r. 481-280.16, Iowa Admin. Code r. 481-280.17, IAC 9/30/26, ARC 9472C effective 9/10/25). `edu-alarm-duties-ia` says installation is "under the rules the director adopts" and its notes say the placement rules were not read. It should add: (1) smoke alarm locations: 'Smoke alarms shall be located on the ceiling or wall outside of each separate sleeping area in the immediate vicinity of bedrooms; in each room used for sleeping purposes; and in each story within a dwelling unit, including basements but not including crawl spaces and uninhabitable attics' (Iowa Admin. Code r. 481-280.15(11)), and 'All existing single-family units and multiple-unit residential buildings shall be equipped with smoke alarms or detectors as required in subrule 280.15(11)' (Iowa Admin. Code r. 481-280.15(13)); (2) equipment: a battery-powered smoke alarm newly installed or replaced after June 30, 2021 'must be powered by a nonremovable, nonreplaceable battery that powers the alarm for at least ten years' (Iowa Admin. Code r. 481-280.15(7)), and hard-wired new or replacement alarms installed after July 1, 2016, where more than one is required, 'will be interconnected' (Iowa Admin. Code r. 481-280.15(7)); (3) carbon monoxide alarm locations: 'In the immediate vicinity of every room used for sleeping purposes in each dwelling unit' (Iowa Admin. Code r. 481-280.17(3)), and in a bedroom or sleeping unit that itself contains a fuel-burning appliance, heater, furnace or fireplace, with the exceptions in Iowa Admin. Code r. 481-280.17(4)-(6); (4) the owner's start-of-tenancy duties: 'to supply and install all required carbon monoxide alarms and to ensure that the batteries are in operating condition at the time the lessee, tenant, guest, or roomer takes possession' (Iowa Admin. Code r. 481-280.17(11)) and 'providing written information regarding carbon monoxide alarm testing and maintenance to one lessee, tenant, guest, or roomer per dwelling unit or sleeping unit' (Iowa Admin. Code r. 481-280.17(11)). Proposed sentences for its body: "Smoke alarms go outside each sleeping area near the bedrooms, in each bedroom, and on every story including the basement. A battery-only smoke alarm installed or replaced since mid-2021 must have a sealed ten-year battery, and hard-wired alarms installed since mid-2016 must be interconnected where more than one is required. Carbon monoxide alarms go near every bedroom (and in a bedroom that has its own fuel-burning appliance or fireplace), unless a listed exception applies. When a tenant moves in, the carbon monoxide alarms must be installed with working batteries, and you must give one tenant in each unit written information on testing and maintaining them." and in notes cite Iowa Admin. Code r. 481-280.15(7), (11), (13) and Iowa Admin. Code r. 481-280.17(3)-(6), (11).
- `smoke-alarm-battery-ia`: Iowa item 7: `smoke-alarm-battery-ia`'s optional sentence "Tenant will provide the batteries for any battery-operated smoke detector or carbon monoxide alarm" tracks Iowa Code § 10A.518(7). Two additions from the rules: (1) for carbon monoxide alarms the rules allow more: 'An owner or manager may require a lessee, tenant, guest, or roomer who has a residency longer than 30 days to be responsible for general maintenance, including but not limited to replacement of any required batteries of the carbon monoxide alarms and testing the carbon monoxide alarms' (Iowa Admin. Code r. 481-280.17(11)), and the tenant 'is responsible for notifying the owner or manager in writing of any deficiencies that the lessee, tenant, guest, or roomer cannot correct and providing the owner or manager access' (Iowa Admin. Code r. 481-280.17(11)); (2) many smoke alarms now have sealed batteries (Iowa Admin. Code r. 481-280.15(7)), so the battery sentence should not ask the tenant to open one. Proposed replacement for the bracketed optional sentence: "[Optional: After Tenant has lived at the property for longer than 30 days, Tenant will replace the batteries in any smoke detector or carbon monoxide alarm that has replaceable batteries and will test the carbon monoxide alarms as the written information Landlord provides describes, and will give Landlord access to correct any problem Tenant cannot correct.]" Also add, outside the brackets: "Landlord has given Tenant written information on testing and maintaining the carbon monoxide alarms." (a duty the owner owes under Iowa Admin. Code r. 481-280.17(11) where carbon monoxide alarms are required; the lease is a convenient but not a required vehicle, so the sentence would be CONDITIONAL on alarms being required).

### §7 Findings for other states

### New {{variables}}

### Proposed SOP changes

1. **Attach the state's earlier log when a gaps prompt relies on it** (Step A / kickoff template). Iowa item 2 asks the pass to turn "IA log §18.2" statuses and the "§6.1" option list into rows, but the 2026-10-02 log was not among the attached files and the earlier workspace was gone, so every topic was answered fresh. Proposed wording for the kickoff template: "If an item cites the state's earlier log, attach that log; the pass checks at Step A that every log it is told to use is attached, and asks for it once if not."
2. **An administrative-code splitter keys on the rule heading, not the rule number** (rule 14, [PR 2] family). Iowa's IAC text starts lines with in-text cross-references such as "481—280.14(10A), a building ..." and "481—280.17(10A)) apply to ...", which a number-only splitter took for rule starts (481-280.14 appeared six times). Require the number, the authority parentheses and a capitalised heading word ("481—280.17(10A) Requirements ..."), and prove the split by listing duplicate rule ids (none after the fix).
3. **Apply errata in the order they were written, not by file name** (rule 80, [RI 5] family). Iowa's errata files were first applied in alphabetical order, so round 2's corrections to sentences that a later-named sweep file had added failed their once-only assertion (two misses, caught by the assertion). The merge now orders errata files by write time. Proposed wording: "The merge applies errata in the order written (by time, or by a sequence number in each line), never by file name."
4. **Write a waiver bar's exception into the brief as one sentence, with what it does not reach.** Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(a)'s farm-home exception was left out of 17 rows by four helpers; a sweep added one uniform sentence, and round 2 then found three rows where that sentence implied a farm-home lease could carry a term another bar still forbids (Iowa Code § 562A.11(1)(d), Iowa Code § 614.1). Proposed: where a state's anti-waiver rule has an exception, class decisions give the exception sentence and the bars it does not lift, and the validator flags any body that cites the bar without it.


### Proposed topic questions

None.

## Federal row: disposing of screening reports (Claude Code, 2026-11-20)

Taylor's decision 2026-11-20 (Washington gaps log §7, backlog item 9): one federal education row, `edu-fcra-disposal-rule`, is tagged here and in every jurisdiction, read from the official text (16 C.F.R. part 682 on eCFR; 15 U.S.C. § 1681w on govinfo.gov). It says that anyone who keeps a screening, credit or background report, or a record taken from one, for a business purpose must dispose of it with reasonable measures against unauthorized access (16 C.F.R. § 682.3), with the rule's examples, and that the rule sets no retention period (§ 682.4). This state's existing rows stay; no clause text changed.

## Tags for situational clauses (Claude Code, 2026-11-22)

Taylor's decision 2026-11-22: every clause shows a tag. A clause that applies only in some situations now carries the label it has when it applies (SOP rule 56): Recommended where the research found no statute requiring it and it serves the landlord, Optional where it is a term the landlord chooses within the law's limits, Required or Legal duty where its notes' statute was read and requires it. No clause text changed.

- `extended-absence-notice-ks`: CONDITIONAL to CONDITIONAL/RECOMMENDED
- `utility-charges-disclosure-ia`: CONDITIONAL to CONDITIONAL/RECOMMENDED
